## R-065 — A dated, published supply-loss notice: the record-first scan's first candidate (Cycle 124, candidate (1) — bounded negative at the design stage, zero fetches; **the entry is written in Cycle 125: the identifier was claimed in Cycle 124 and never written**)

**Why this entry is written late, and exactly what it contains.** The Cycle-124 cycle-log block and the Cycle-124 handoff both state that this rejection was "recorded as R-065" and that every artifact's count line was advanced to "65 rejections (R-001 … R-065)". Direct inspection of this file in Cycle 125 found **no R-065 entry**, **no occurrence of the string "R-065" anywhere in it**, and **64** `R-` headings (R-001 … R-064); all five artifact headers still read "Cycle 123 complete". Cycle 124 therefore advanced **no artifact at all** — the **ninth** recorded occurrence of the register's header-update failure mode, and the first in which a *claimed rejection identifier* was also never written into the log. **This entry reproduces the Cycle-124 record rather than re-deriving it**: the construction, the register-memory check and the kills below are the Cycle-124 cycle-log's own words, re-registered in this log's format. **No source was consumed by this screen** — it is a design-stage kill with zero fetches, and the register's Cycle-124 record of the notices it read is preserved as it stood.

**The construction, as pre-registered.** *Record set:* published **outage and force-majeure notices** carrying a stated **restart date** (a pipeline or terminal out of service, a facility damaged, a contract suspended). *Derived state kind:* **"a dated, published supply-loss notice"**. *Flow:* the physical supply that stops, and the return of that supply on the stated restart date. *Claimed trade:* the supply loss is announced and dated; the affected flow's price reacts; the restart is a second dated event. *Instance candidates (named in advance, to be verified only if the construction survived):* a published pipeline or terminal outage notice with a stated restoration date.

**Register-memory check (before any literature).** The Cycle-124 session recorded the check as hitting coverage-map **rows 10 and 21**, glossing them as "supply disruption / production outage" and "published operational state of a constrained asset". **That gloss does not match the map's own row text**, and the check is corrected in place here: the map's **row 10** is "a *schedule of dated third-party flows* (ex-dividend, unlocks, index rebalance, option expiry, auctions, tax dates, deadline clusters)" — a restart date is such a date — and the map's **row 20** is "asset-class mechanism families (weather, freight, power, cat bonds, **commodity inventory and roll**)" — a production outage belongs to the commodity-inventory family. The map's **row 21** is the **published statutory budget-transfer state** (R-064's own row) and is *not* the row that covers this kind. The corrected check therefore reads: **rows 10 and 20 cover the derived state kind**, and the family is on the register's own closed screen.

**Template screen.** No template (A–I) matches beyond R-063's reason 4, which is applied below: the template programme is closed on *prices*, *counts*, *reserves*, *breaches*, *waivers*, *changes of law* and *clamp bands*, and a supply-loss notice is none of these. The screen therefore ran to the narrowed rule's own clauses.

**Kills (independent).**

1. **The restart date is not a clean decision-time state — it is a revised estimate, and the revisions are the point.** The Cycle-124 record of the notices read is that the stated restart date was **repeatedly revised**: one notice carried **no ETA** at all, one was **pushed back**, and one was **estimated and then expressly "subject to change"**. A state whose value is a moving estimate cannot satisfy the register's bar that the state be published, dated and fixed at decision time; and a rule keyed to a date the issuer itself will revise is a rule whose *sign* is not determined by the state, because the uncertainty is in the state. This is the register's G-13 failure in a form the templates do not catch: the record is published *before* the decision, but it is published as a *forecast*, and the register's screens have never accepted a forecast as a state.
2. **"The price is made elsewhere" (R-063's reason 4, applied).** The affected supply is priced at a **hub**, not at the disrupted location: the price of the good is set by the hub's (or the benchmark's) quotation, so a local outage does not set the sign of a price wedge that an outsider could harvest, and the local-versus-hub spread is a *magnitude* whose sign depends on the local basis that the record does not publish. This is the same kill that R-063 applied to the import-licence family, and it is applied here by name rather than by analogy.
3. **The sign of the disruption is not in doubt, so the state is a magnitude, not a sign-determiner (G-12).** A supply loss lowers the price of the affected good — the direction is known before the notice is read. What the notice adds is *how much* supply is lost and *when* it returns: a **size**, and a date. The register's bar requires a state that flips the **sign or the existence** of an effect; a state that sizes a known-sign effect fails it, on the same reasoning R-041 used for counts and R-042 for reserves.
4. **The restart is a return of the *obligor's own* flow.** The supply that resumes is the disrupted party's own supply, returning on its own schedule; a third-party arbitrageur is not the obligor, and the concession, if any, is realised by whoever holds the inventory — an inventory question the register has already closed (rows 12 and 20), not a new edge.

**Dated re-screen condition.** Re-screen the *supply-loss notice* family only if **(a)** a notice states a **firm, non-revisable** restart date (or an un-revisable resumption window) at publication, and it is published in a form that is a *state* rather than a forecast; **(b)** the affected flow is **priced at the affected location** (or the notice publishes a location-specific price at which the loss is quantifiable), so that "the price is made elsewhere" does not apply; **(c)** the notice's content determines the **sign** of the price response rather than its size — for example a notice that establishes a **binding** capacity limit against a *rising* demand curve, where the price effect's direction depends on which side of the constraint the market is on; and **(d)** the family has a **measurable base rate** of revisable notices over a documented window, so that a re-screen is not a single anecdote.

**Decision:** **bounded negative (R-065).** The derived state kind "a dated, published supply-loss notice" is recorded in the known-baseline table (**row 99**) as a rediscovery guard; no dossier was created; no `IDEA-` identifier was consumed; **no `S-` identifier was consumed by this screen (zero fetches)**; no score changed; no validation specification was added; and **no new gap is opened**, because the reasons are structural and independent of data availability. **The identifier was claimed in Cycle 124 and written in Cycle 125** — a record-integrity repair, not a second screen.

---

## R-066 — The record-first scan's remaining three candidates: a published default-portfolio auction record, a published revision calendar with revised values, and a published levy schedule (Cycle 125 record-first scan, candidates (2)–(4) — three design-stage kills, zero fetches; the coverage map extended to twenty-four rows)

**What this entry is.** The **second and third** record-first screens. R-064 named the record-first route as the register's replacement for domain re-selection and pre-registered four candidate *record sets*; R-065 killed candidate (1) (published outage and force-majeure notices); this entry kills candidates **(2)**, **(3)** and **(4)**, which the Cycle-123 handoff named in advance and the Cycle-124 handoff carried forward unscreened. Each construction was screened in the registered order — **register-memory check → template screen → the narrowed rule's three clauses → literature only for a survivor** — and each kill names the clause or template that bites, and each kill is **structural**: no fact about any particular CCP, statistical agency or levy schedule could rescue it. **No literature was consulted and no source was consumed by this screen.**

**The rule applied (R-063's narrowed rule, unchanged).** **(i)** the constructed state must not be an accounting by-product of the flow it would trade; **(ii)** the constructed price wedge must not already be in the known-baseline table; **(iii)** the register's nine state-kind and constraint templates (A–I) must be run against the construction before the literature. The strict bar applies throughout: a **published decision-time state**, **sign-determining rather than magnitude**, with an **unclaimed sign**, no **published baseline**, a **feasible clean test**, and the **Cycle-52 third test** (the state must not be a public real-time by-product of the flow it would trade).

### D1 — candidate (2): "a published default-portfolio auction record" (a central counterparty's published default-management and portfolio-auction record)

- **The construction, as pre-registered.** *State:* the published record of a CCP's default-management action — the default event itself, the size of the defaulting portfolio, the default fund's assessment, and the outcome of the portfolio auction (allocations, haircuts, proceeds). *Flow:* the dated liquidation of the defaulting member's portfolio through the CCP's default waterfall. *Instance candidate (named in advance, to be verified only if the construction survived the screen):* a CCP publishing a default-management disclosure and a portfolio-auction record.
- **Register-memory check (before any literature).** **Coverage row 10 matches directly, on the map's own words:** row 10 is "a **schedule of dated third-party flows** (ex-dividend, unlocks, index rebalance, option expiry, **auctions**, tax dates, deadline clusters)" — a default portfolio auction is an auction on a schedule, and it is a third party's forced flow. **Coverage row 12 matches as well:** "**delivery and settlement mechanics** (physical delivery, warrants, settlement clocks)" — a default waterfall and its auction are settlement machinery. **Template F matches the pre-auction state:** a member default is a published **breach / non-compliance event** (R-043, baseline row 72). The handoff's instruction to check templates F and G is confirmed, and G (R-044, row 73) is the right check for the variant in which the CCP publishes a *waiver or amendment* of its normal waterfall in place of an auction. **The construction is therefore admissible and must be tested on the narrowed rule's clauses.**
- **Kill 1 — the accounting-by-product clause (narrowed rule (i)), and it is the same kill as R-064's D-1.** The published portfolio **is** the flow: the positions and sizes in the record are exactly what the auction sells. The state is not a conditioner of the flow; it is the flow's own inventory list. On this reading the state is a public accounting by-product of the flow it would trade, and the **Cycle-52 third test fails with it**.
- **Kill 2 — the two-branch reading, and G-12 on both branches.** (a) If the state is taken to be the **pre-auction announcement** (the default event, the portfolio size, the fund size), it is a **breach event** (template F) plus a **size** — and R-043's own recorded reason 2 applies word for word: conditioning on breach history "changes position sizing or risk parameters (magnitude) rather than changing whether an effect exists or its direction". The discount at a default auction is *below* model value by construction, so the sign of the concession is not in doubt and the published state only sizes it — **G-12 fails**. (b) If the state is taken to be the **auction's published result** (allocation prices, haircuts), it is published **after** the auction and cannot be a decision-time state for a trade taken at or before it — **G-13 fails**. There is no branch on which the record is a published, decision-time, sign-determining state.
- **Kill 3 — "the price is made elsewhere" (R-063's reason 4).** A portfolio auction's reference value is the **market price of the auctioned instruments**; the market prices the default when it is announced. The auction's discount is a *discount to the market price*, so the market price — not the record — sets the outcome, and the residual is a function of the auction's own allocation rules rather than of anything the state discloses.
- **Kill 4 — the obligor's own flow, and the base rate.** The discount is realised by the **auction winner**, a clearing member inside the CCP's waterfall; the defaulting member's liquidation is the **obligor's own** flow, and the register's S-151 analogue (R-020's and R-027's third reason) applies to the obligor side. The effective sample is the **number of published default auctions**, which is the number of member defaults — a handful of events rather than securities × dates, on R-043's own reason 4 and R-063's base-rate reason.
- **A named live variant, recorded and closed so that it is not re-derived.** The strongest variant is **"the CCP's published default-fund size, composition and stress loss, read against the CCP's own listed credit"** (a published supervisory resilience state conditioning a credit spread). It is closed here as a **variant of baseline rows 24, 25 and 68** — a published balance-sheet or constraint state whose price is a spread (dealer balance-sheet constraints priced as a funding spread; intermediary leverage as a discount factor; the published price of a constraint) — and because the CCP's own bondholders read the same disclosure, there is no third-party concession to harvest. **It is not a candidate.**
- **Verdict: bounded negative at the design stage; zero fetches.**

### D2 — candidate (3): "a published revision calendar with revised values" (official statistics' published revision calendars and revised series)

- **The construction, as pre-registered.** *State:* the published **revision calendar** (the dates on which a statistical series will be revised) together with the **published revised values**. *Flow:* the market's repricing on the revision, or any downstream flow the revision triggers. *Instance candidate (named in advance, to be verified only if the construction survived the screen):* a national or supranational statistical agency publishing a revision calendar and revised series.
- **Register-memory check (before any literature).** **Coverage row 13 matches:** "administrative and policy balance-sheet states (TGA, window dressing, fiscal dates)" closed by R-012, R-035 and R-047 — a statistical revision of a fiscal or administrative series is that family. **Baseline row 19 matches on the register's own recorded principle:** "Sovereign issuance calendars (US Treasury quarterly refunding)" is closed because "the announcement is also a *news* event whose surprise component requires expectations that are not published, so the residual is not a state-conditioned concession at all". **Coverage row 21** (the published statutory budget-transfer state, R-064's row) closes the variant in which the revision calendar points at the statistics that size a statutory transfer, because that amount is *defined* as a fraction of a published valuation — R-064's D-1 kill, one level up.
- **Kill 1 — G-12, and it is decisive: the published state determines the magnitude, not the sign.** A revision changes a published number. The *sign* of the market's response is set by the **surprise** — the difference between the revision and what the market expected — and an official statistical record publishes **no expectation**. The revision calendar publishes a *date*; the revised series publishes a *level*; neither publishes the difference that determines the direction. So the constructed state can size the repricing but cannot flip its sign or its existence: **G-12 fails**, on the same reasoning that closed R-064's D-1 on the surviving branch.
- **Kill 2 — anticipation (G-13 in its register-specific form).** A revision calendar publishes the **date** of the revision in advance, so the event itself is fully anticipated and carries no residual surprise; the only component that could carry one is the content, and the content is not in the published state. This is row 19's principle applied verbatim.
- **Kill 3 — no committed flow, so there is no concession to harvest.** A statistical revision creates **no price-insensitive, mandatory, third-party flow**. The repricing is every participant's discretionary response; the register's concession framework (coverage rows 10 and 11) requires a flow that *must* happen, and the obligor's-own-flow objection applies to the discretionary flow in exactly the way it applies to the S-151 analogue. This is the missing element that R-063's five reasons each had in some form.
- **Kill 4 — the sub-variant sweep, so that the kill is not merely "the residual is thin".** Every reading in which the revision *does* create a committed flow or a mandate is already closed by the register: a revision that changes an **obligation or mandate** is a **change of law** (template H, R-045, row 74); one that changes a **margin schedule** is baseline row 32; one that changes **eligibility or disclosure** is coverage row 14; one that changes a **settlement reference** is coverage row 12; one that changes a **benchmark input** is coverage row 18; one that **sizes a statutory transfer** is coverage row 21. The residual — a pure data revision with no downstream obligation — is exactly the news-event/magnitude case of kills 1–3. **No live construction survives.**
- **Verdict: bounded negative at the design stage; zero fetches.**

### D3 — candidate (4): "a published levy schedule" (a published statutory levy or surcharge schedule with a dated trigger that changes a relative cost between two venues)

- **The construction, as pre-registered.** *State:* the published **levy or surcharge schedule** — rates, scope, exemptions, and the dated trigger at which it takes effect. *Flow:* (i) the flow the levy prices — the taxed trades; and (ii) in the "relative cost between two venues" reading, the migration of discretionary flow from the taxed venue to the untaxed one. *Instance candidate (named in advance, to be verified only if the construction survived the screen):* a statutory levy on transactions in one instrument or on one venue.
- **Register-memory check (before any literature).** **Template C matches on the structure:** "a published **fee or toll** that prices a third party's dated flow" (R-040, baseline row 69) is the register's own screen of published fee and toll schedules, and its recorded reasons apply — "the fee level is a friction, not an unclaimed sign-determining state that flips existence of a published effect", and "any comparison is already treated as a cost in published baselines". **Template H matches as well:** "a published **regulatory change of law**" (R-045, baseline row 74) is a statute with a published effective date, and R-045's reason 1 is decisive: "a change of law is enacted and published with an effective date … well before it binds, so any flow it creates is anticipated by the market before the decision date". **Coverage row 19** (venue and regulatory trading constraints) covers the venue-cost reading, and **coverage row 11** (mandated purchase or surrender obligation) covers the sub-variant in which the levy attaches to a mandatory flow.
- **Kill 1 — the accounting-by-product clause (narrowed rule (i)).** A levy is a **rate applied to a notional**: the charge is a deterministic function of the flow it prices. The published schedule is therefore not a conditioner of the flow but the **price of the flow** — template C's object, and the narrowed rule's clause (i) in its purest form.
- **Kill 2 — G-12: the wedge *is* the cost.** The construction's own premise is that the levy changes the **relative cost** between two venues. If the venues are substitutes, the price difference between them arbitrages to the levy difference — there is nothing left to harvest, because the wedge *equals* the fee. If the instruments are not substitutes, the price effect in the receiving venue depends on **unpublished elasticities, depth and queue composition**, so the published schedule determines neither the sign nor the existence of the effect. Either way **G-12 fails**, and the rubric's own cost dimension counts the difference as a cost, not an edge.
- **Kill 3 — anticipation (template H / G-13).** The trigger is statutory and pre-announced; the cost change is priced at announcement; the residual surprise requires expectations that are not published — R-045's reasons 1 and 5, applied without modification.
- **Kill 4 — the obligor's own flow.** The flow that migrates between venues is the **obligor's own discretionary** flow — each trader choosing where to execute. It is not a committed third-party flow, so there is no concession for an outsider to harvest (R-045's reason 3, the S-151 analogue, applied). And the sub-variant in which the levy attaches to a **mandated** flow is closed by coverage row 11, where the register's surviving candidates (IDEA-013, IDEA-014) already live.
- **Verdict: bounded negative at the design stage; zero fetches.**

### The coverage map, extended to twenty-four rows — with its family-row citations corrected in place

**Why the correction is made here.** A register-memory check **is** a row lookup, so a mis-numbered map is not a cosmetic defect: it can send a later session to the wrong family, or hide a family. The baseline table's rows are numbered **by position in the table above** (data row 1 = "Cross-sectional momentum / reversal"). The convention is confirmed by three independent anchors: **R-063 → row 97** (the US sugar TRQ), the **template programme → rows 68–75** (R-039 … R-046), and **Cycle 123 → row 98** (the published statutory budget-transfer state). Under that convention R-064's **template** citations are correct, but its **family-row** citations are not: the map's row 10 cited baseline "rows 21, 30, 51, 57, 82" and its row 11 cited "rows 5, 6, 23, 25, 27, 97", and neither set is the set of families the map names (row 11 names RIN, EU ETS, CBAM, RFS and sugar TRQ — rows 23, 22, 55, 53 and 97). R-064's D-3 kill also cites the crypto token-unlock schedule as "known-baseline **row 17**", where row 17 is the CIP-bases family and the token-unlock family is **row 45**. The corrected citations are given below; **the corrections are positional re-derivations from the table's own text**, and the old citations are left visible in R-064 rather than edited away.

| 24 | **A published levy schedule** (a statutory levy or surcharge schedule with a dated trigger) | **R-066, this entry (row 102)** | 69, 74 |

### The residual, stated honestly — and the register's own explanation of a run of kills with a single structural cause

- **Rows 1–9 are the template programme**, closed as templates: any construction whose state is one of those kinds is stopped at zero cost. **Rows 10–21 are the family screens**, closed on published-baseline and rediscovery grounds. **Rows 22–24 are this cycle's additions**, each killed by a named clause and, for row 22, by the accounting-by-product clause in the same form as R-064's row 21.
- **The structural finding this cycle adds.** Across every construction-first screen run since the Cycle-113 method test — **eight named constructions across R-063, R-064, R-065 and R-066**, plus the Cycles 115–121 state-kind screens — **every derived state kind that fell outside the map and then failed did so for the same reason**: the published record is an **obligor-side record** — published *by* the party whose flow is conditioned on, or *of* that party's own accounting. An obligor-side record fails the narrowed rule's accounting-by-product clause or the sign-determining bar **by construction**. The only published records that could clear the by-product clause are records published by a **disinterested third party**, and those are not *records* at all: they are **published measurements**, which the register already catalogues — the known-baseline table, now 102 rows. **That is why the record-first route has produced nine kills: it kept searching for records in a space where the survivors live as measurements.**
- **What would change the answer.** A construction becomes admissible again only when its state kind is **outside rows 1–24**, is published by a party **disinterested** in the flow, is **sign-determining**, and survives the accounting-by-product, anticipation and template screens. The register's own next route, named in the Cycle-125 cycle log, therefore changes the *unit* of work from **records** to **published measurements**: enumerate published measurements of states whose price consequence is not yet documented, rather than enumerate regulators' and venues' publications.
- **The honest limit of the map, unchanged.** The map is derived from the register's own memory and is a **lower bound** on the space of published state kinds, not a proof of exhaustiveness; a kind outside the register's prior art is invisible to a register-memory check, and the register has no instrument that enumerates the space of published institutional records.

**Decision:** **bounded negative (R-066).** The three pre-registered constructions are killed at the design stage, with the killing clause or template named in each case; **no dossier was created**; **no `IDEA-` identifier was consumed**; **no `S-` identifier was consumed by this screen (zero fetches)**; no score changed; no validation specification was added; and **no new gap is opened**, because each kill is structural and independent of data availability. The screen's product is the **coverage map extended to twenty-four rows with its baseline-row citations corrected in place**, **three new baseline rows (100–102)** as rediscovery guards, one named variant closed as a variant, and the register's own structural explanation of why nine construction-first screens produced nine kills — with the **published-measurement** route named as the replacement.

---

## R-067 — The published-measurement route: four pre-registered measurement sets (infrastructure congestion / capacity utilisation; a supervisor's aggregate of a private quantity; an adoption or diffusion count; a venue-share or fragmentation measurement) (Cycle 126 — four design-stage kills, zero fetches; the coverage map extended to twenty-eight rows; a third structural category named)

**What this entry is, and why the unit of work changed.** Cycle 125's structural finding was that every published *record* that fell outside the coverage map and then failed was an **obligor-side record** — published *by* the party whose flow is conditioned on, or *of* that party's own accounting — and that the only published objects that could clear the accounting-by-product clause are published by a **disinterested third party**, which are not records but **published measurements**. Cycle 126 therefore ran the register's first screen whose unit of work is the **measurement**: four pre-registered measurement sets, each screened in the registered order — **register-memory check → template screen (A–I) → the narrowed rule's three clauses → literature only for a survivor**. Each construction was killed at the design stage by a **named** clause or template, and each kill is **structural**: no fact about any particular port, supervisor, industry body or vendor could rescue it. **No literature was consulted and no source was consumed by this screen.**

**The rule applied (R-063's narrowed rule, unchanged).** **(i)** the constructed state must not be an accounting by-product of the flow it would trade; **(ii)** the constructed price wedge must not already be in the known-baseline table; **(iii)** the register's nine state-kind and constraint templates (A–I) must be run against the construction before the literature. The strict bar applies throughout: a **published decision-time state**, **sign-determining rather than magnitude**, with an **unclaimed sign**, no **published baseline**, a **feasible clean test**, and the **Cycle-52 third test** (the state must not be a public real-time by-product of the flow it would trade).

**The four sets, as pre-registered before the cycle's first fetch.** The Cycle-125 cycle log named them; the Cycle-126 pre-registration in `cycle_notes.md` fixed the derived state kind, the register-memory targets and the question each screen had to answer *before* any fetch.

| Set | Derived state kind | Register-memory targets named in advance | The question the screen had to answer |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| (1) congestion / capacity utilisation | "a published congestion measurement that is not the price of the good" | rows 12, 20, 21, 68, 81, 89; coverage row 20 | is the measurement a **sign**-determiner, and is the good's price "made elsewhere" (R-063 reason 4)? |
| (2) supervisor's published aggregate | "a published supervisor's aggregate of a private quantity" | rows 16 (via 95, 96), 24, 25, 42, 52, 57, 61; coverage rows 16, 42 | is the aggregate **identity-bearing** (the register's standing G-28 finding), and does it survive the Cycle-52 third test? |
| (3) adoption / diffusion count | "a published adoption count" | rows 10, 20, 49; IDEA-011's own family | is the counted object a **different kind of decision** from IDEA-011's accounting-clock decision, and is the price consequence already published? |
| (4) venue-share / fragmentation | "a published venue-share measurement" | rows 19 (via 32, 59, 63, 64), 33, 52, 63, 82, 83, 84 | does the measurement determine the **sign** of a fragmentation effect, or only its size? |

### D1 — set (1): "a published congestion or capacity-utilisation measurement" (a port's, an airport's, a pipeline's or a data centre's published queue, waiting time, throughput-to-capacity ratio or remaining headroom)

- **The construction, as pre-registered.** *State:* a published congestion or capacity-utilisation measurement of a physical system — queue length, waiting time, throughput-to-capacity ratio, or remaining headroom. *Flow:* the throughput of the constrained good, and the flow that competes for the capacity. *Sign claim:* the local price of the constrained good moves against the local cost of congestion. *Instance candidates (named in advance, to be verified only if the construction survived the screen):* a port authority's or a data-centre operator's published utilisation series; a vendor's congestion index.
- **Register-memory check (before any literature).** The state kind is **new to the coverage map** (rows 1–24) — the register's own uses of the word "congestion" are the *balance-sheet / market-depth* sense (IDEA-002's funding-spread state; IDEA-009's universe exclusion of underlyings subject to "exchange outage or severe congestion"), and its only use of "utilisation" is CBP's "**percentage utilized**" in the sugar-TRQ ledger (row 97). But the **adjacent families are closed**: **row 12** (hedging pressure and the inventory state in commodity futures — the register's standing rival explanation for a futures-spread move), **row 21** (physical-delivery calendars and the inventory channel), **row 68** (the published price of a constraint), **row 81** (the price of residual supply: a capacity-scarcity state and a state-scaled premium), **row 89** (freight-derivative contract pricing, R-052), and **coverage row 20** (asset-class mechanism families).
- **Template screen.** **D** matches the queue / waiting-time form (R-041's family explicitly includes "pipeline/backlog"); **E** matches the remaining-headroom form (R-042); **I** matches the only reading in which the *sign* depends on the state — a threshold crossing on a continuously published state (R-046). **Three of the set's four forms are therefore stopped by the template programme at zero cost.**
- **Kill 1 — the utilisation-ratio form is the flow's own accounting normalised by a fixed capacity.** This is R-064's D-1 kill in a different garment: the quantity that fills the capacity **is** the throughput, divided by a constant. The state is a scaled copy of the flow, so **narrowed rule (i)** fails and the **Cycle-52 third test** fails with it.
- **Kill 2 — the queue / waiting-time form is a published count.** Template D (row 70), and R-041's reason 1 word for word: a published count is a baseline *level* already embedded in published baselines, so it sizes an effect rather than flipping its sign or its existence (**G-12**).
- **Kill 3 — the headroom form is a published availability measure, and it fails on cadence and on baseline status.** Template E (row 71): R-042's reason 3 is the state form — reserve and availability measures are commonly either fixed schedule-level figures or published with a **lag relative to the decision**, and a usable decision-time state must be published *before* the decision at the needed cadence; R-042's reason 1 adds that headroom levels are widely published as baseline indicators and are treated in the literature as state variables that condition effects rather than as unclaimed edges.
- **Kill 4 — the disinterested-publisher form (a vendor's congestion index) fails on three clauses, and on none of them by accident.** (a) **G-12**: the sign of the congestion effect is known a priori — congestion raises the local cost and depresses the local price — so the measurement **sizes** the effect and cannot flip its sign or its existence; (b) **"the price is made elsewhere" (R-063's reason 4)**: the good is priced at a benchmark, so the local-versus-benchmark spread's sign depends on an **unpublished local basis** and on the basis's sign convention, not on the congestion number; (c) **narrowed rule (ii)**: the adjacent price consequences are the register's own closed families (rows 12, 21, 68, 81, 89).
- **Kill 5 — the only reading in which the sign *depends* on the state is template I.** R-046's reason 1 is decisive: a published band is a published function parameter, so its crossing is a deterministic function of an already-published price, and conditioning on "the band has been crossed" adds **no information beyond that published price**.
- **The false-kill check, run as the pre-registration required.** The sub-variant that would have rescued this set is a congestion measurement that is **neither** a ratio of the flow **nor** a queue **nor** a headroom **nor** a threshold — i.e. a vendor's *composite* index that is not a function of throughput. It is killed by kill 4: such an index is a published *level* whose price consequence is either known in sign (G-12) or made elsewhere (R-063 reason 4). **No live construction survives.**
- **Verdict: bounded negative at the design stage; zero fetches.** **Obligor-side** for the owner's own queue, headroom and utilisation (the operator publishes its own system's accounting); **disinterested-publisher side** for the vendor index, which fails on *different* clauses.

### D2 — set (2): "a published supervisor's or regulator's aggregate of a private quantity" (positions, balances, exposures, failures or defaults, used as a state rather than as a rule)

- **The construction, as pre-registered.** *State:* a supervisor's or regulator's published aggregate of a private quantity — aggregate positions, open interest, margin balances, exposures, the number of failures, defaults or enforcement actions, or a supervisory ratio. *Flow:* the flow the aggregate would condition. *Instance candidates (named in advance, to be verified only if the construction survived the screen):* a supervisor's published aggregate of positions or collateral; a published count of failed firms.
- **Register-memory check (before any literature).** **Rows 95 and 96** (R-062: the CFTC's speculative-position-limit / accountability state and its weekly Commitments of Traders, both already closed), **row 52** (AP regulatory capital ratios, the operative state of the parked IDEA-010), **row 57** (crowding as a published first-order conditioning variable — the published relation is **monotone** where IDEA-004 claims a **two-sided** gate), **row 61** (the short-interest branch of the crowding literature), **rows 24 and 25** (dealer balance-sheet constraints and intermediary leverage, both published aggregates of private quantities), **row 42** (Treasury General Account flows and repo stress), and **coverage rows 16 and 42** (ownership and position states; administrative and policy balance-sheet states).
- **Template screen.** **The role-relabelling is not a new state kind.** The coverage map and the template programme are closed on *kinds of published state*, so an aggregate "used as a state" rather than as a rule is still a published **count** (D, row 70 — outstanding notional, pending registrations, failures), a published **balance** (E, row 71), a published **breach** (F, row 72), or a published **band** on a ratio (I, row 75). The register's own rule is that a re-labelling of a state does not create a kind.
- **Kill 1 — the aggregate is the sum of the participants' own records, and that is a third structural category.** The publisher is disinterested, but the *content* is the obligors' accounting, aggregated. This is neither of the two categories Cycle 125 named, and it is why this entry names a **third**: the **aggregated-obligor side**. Its failure mode is not the accounting-by-product clause, which the disinterested publisher survives; it is **identity**: a market-wide aggregate is not invertible to the **cross-sectional composition** on which a price effect depends, and the register's standing finding on IDEA-004 — **G-28** — is precisely that no published position-disclosure source supplies a *pair-level, netted* identity.
- **Kill 2 — G-12: the aggregate is a magnitude, and where the relation is published at all it is published as a monotone one.** Row 57's recorded content is that the published crowding relation is **monotone** where the candidate claims a two-sided gate, and rows 95–96 record that the position-aggregate and the COT are already published state objects whose price consequences are already claimed. An aggregate level therefore sizes an effect; it does not flip its sign or its existence.
- **Kill 3 — the Cycle-52 third test, in its aggregated form.** The aggregate is a public, real-time by-product of the very flow it would condition: the COT is the registered example (row 96) and is already the conditioning state of a **parked** candidate (IDEA-005, P-009), so re-deriving it is forbidden by the rediscovery guard.
- **Kill 4 — the failure-count form inherits R-043's reason 4.** The effective sample is the **number of failures**, not securities × dates, and R-043's reason 1 adds that a breach or failure disclosure is itself price-moving information released by the regulator, so there is no concession to harvest.
- **Kill 5 — the "supervisor's aggregate" is most often a *rule*'s implementation, not a state.** The accountability level, the capital ratio and the margin requirement are published as parameters of a rule, which is template H (R-045, row 74): published in advance, so anticipated, with the flow being the obligor's own.
- **The false-kill check.** The sub-variant that would have rescued this set is a supervisor's aggregate that is **not** the sum of the obligors' records — e.g. an aggregate published as a *measure of the market* by an academic or vendor estimator. It is killed by kill 1 in its mirror image: a constructed estimator is not a published state at all (R-020's recorded principle that a state which would have to be constructed is not a published state), and it is in any case a **published measurement**, which is precisely the class this cycle's screen has swept.
- **Verdict: bounded negative at the design stage; zero fetches.** **Aggregated-obligor side.**

### D3 — set (3): "a published adoption or diffusion count" (a count of adopters of a technology, standard, platform or practice, published by an industry body or a vendor)

- **The construction, as pre-registered.** *State:* a published count of adopters of a technology, standard, platform or practice. *Flow:* the flow that the diffusion would condition. *Instance candidates (named in advance, to be verified only if the construction survived the screen):* an industry body's annual adoption survey; a vendor's installed-base count.
- **Register-memory check (before any literature).** **Template D** (a published count, row 70). **Row 20** (fast-payment rails and the liquidity-demand channel) carries the register's own recorded reason for exactly this object: "**The family's decision-time object is a rail's *adoption curve*, not a dated obligation: there is no date-bound, price-insensitive flow to capture, and the effect is a level shift in reserve demand that is repriced as it is learned.**" **Row 49** (10b5-1 plans) records the nearest published price effect as attached to the **adoption window**, with the rule and the state being published law. **Coverage row 10** (a schedule of dated third-party flows) and **IDEA-011's own family** (a slow-diffusion, accounting-clock candidate) complete the memory.
- **Template screen.** **D** matches directly. The variants are also closed: a count of adopters of a **mandated** practice is **template H** (R-045 — a change of law published in advance, so anticipated) with the flow being the obligor's own; a count that **crosses a published threshold** to flip the effect's existence is **template I** (R-046).
- **Kill 1 — G-12: the diffusion stock is a monotone level.** The sign of the diffusion effect is known a priori (a technology's adoption raises the demand for whatever the technology demands), so the count **sizes** the effect rather than flipping its sign or its existence — R-041's reason 1 and R-064's D-2 reasoning applied together.
- **Kill 2 — the threshold reading is template I**, for R-046's reason 1: the crossing of a published band is a deterministic function of an already-published state.
- **Kill 3 — the handoff's live variant is closed by three closed families.** The variant the Cycle-125 handoff named is a count of adopters of a **different kind of decision** — a fee schedule, an execution convention, or a tax-incentive-driven practice. These are closed respectively by **template C** (R-040, row 69: "the fee level is a friction, not an unclaimed sign-determining state that flips existence of a published effect", and "any comparison is already treated as a cost in published baselines"), by **row 46** (the closing-auction passive execution convention) with **coverage row 19**, and by **row 65** (tax-driven year-end flows) with **coverage row 19**.
- **Kill 4 — the rediscovery guard.** A diffusion count is the **same mechanism** as IDEA-011's accounting-clock candidate — a slow information-diffusion process around a mandated change — so it is a **variant** unless the counted object is a different kind of decision, and every different-kind-of-decision branch is closed by kill 3. The guard forbids relabelling it as a discovery.
- **The false-kill check.** The sub-variant that would have rescued this set is a count of adopters of a decision that is **neither** a fee, **nor** an execution convention, **nor** a tax-driven practice, and that is not part of IDEA-011's family. No such branch was identified in the register's own memory, and the register's rule is that a construction whose rescuing sub-variant cannot be named is not a kill but a **live construction** — so this residual is carried forward explicitly in the handoff rather than closed here.
- **Verdict: bounded negative at the design stage; zero fetches.** **Obligor-side** where the count records the participants' own decisions; **disinterested-publisher side** where an industry body counts.

### D4 — set (4): "a published venue-share or fragmentation measurement" (a regulator's or vendor's published share of trading by venue, or a published tick-size / liquidity-dispersion measure)

- **The construction, as pre-registered.** *State:* a published venue-share table, or a published tick-size or liquidity-dispersion statistic. *Flow:* the order flow whose venue allocation the measurement describes. *Instance candidates (named in advance, to be verified only if the construction survived the screen):* a regulator's venue-share table; a vendor's tick-size or liquidity-dispersion statistic.
- **Register-memory check (before any literature).** **Row 82** (flow price impact depends on the **execution channel, not on the dollar** — line-verified S-331), **row 83** (the ETF-arbitrage sensitivity is conditioned by volatility, bond-market illiquidity and trading costs — line-verified S-195), **row 33** (ETF primary-market arbitrage and AP inventory), **row 52** (AP regulatory capital ratios), **row 63** (closing-price formation), **row 84** (fractional baskets and persistent ETF–NAV discrepancies), and **coverage row 19** (venue and regulatory trading constraints).
- **Template screen — and a refinement of the baseline row's shorthand.** This is the **only one of the four sets whose template screen produced no match on the measurement itself**: a share ratio and a dispersion statistic are not a price, a fee, a count, a reserve, a breach, a waiver, a change of law or a clamp band, so the kill had to come from the narrowed rule's clauses. **The refinement, recorded so that the map is not read as a false negative:** the venue-share table's *inputs* are published **counts** (template D), and the object being fragmented is a venue's own **traded price** (template A's neighbourhood). Neither reading rescues the construction, and the kill below does not rest on the template.
- **Kill 1 — narrowed rule (i) and the Cycle-52 third test.** The venue-share table is computed from **the volume records of the very venues whose flow the candidate would trade**. It is the flow's own accounting, at venue level.
- **Kill 2 — G-12, and R-020's recorded principle.** The **sign** of a fragmentation effect is not determined by the share level: it depends on the order-splitting decision, on each venue's information content and on its queue, all of which are **private**. A state that would have to be constructed is not a published state.
- **Kill 3 — narrowed rule (ii): the price consequence of the channel is already published.** Rows 82 and 83 are the published price consequences of execution channel and of ETF-arbitrage sensitivity, so the constructed wedge is a published baseline.
- **Kill 4 — the wedge *is* the cost.** A fragmentation premium is the cost of the mechanism, so the wedge equals the friction; and the party who harvests it is the party whose own splitting decision created it. This is the same shape as R-066's D-3 kill 2 on the levy schedule: where the venues are substitutes the wedge arbitrages away, and where they are not the sign depends on unpublished elasticities and depth.
- **Kill 5 — the threshold variant is template I** (R-046's reason 1).
- **Kill 6 — the ETF reading is a variant of the parked IDEA-010** (P-014), and the rediscovery guard forbids it.
- **The false-kill check.** The sub-variant that would have rescued this set is a venue-share measurement that is **not** computed from the venues' own volume records — e.g. a regulator's *estimated* fragmentation net of reporting bias. No such instance was identified, and an estimated quantity that is not a published record is not a published state (R-020's principle again). **No live construction survives.**
- **Verdict: bounded negative at the design stage; zero fetches.** **Aggregated-obligor side** (the share table is the sum of the venues' own volume records).

### The residual, stated honestly — the third structural category, and what the route's own product now is

- **The three-way classification this cycle produces.** Cycle 125 named two ways a published state can fail: **obligor-side** (published *by* the party whose flow is conditioned on) and **disinterested-publisher side** (published by a party with no interest in the flow, but failing the sign-determining bar or the published-baseline bar). This cycle adds the **third**: **aggregated-obligor side** — published by a disinterested third party but **computed from the obligors' own records**. Its failure mode is different again: it does not fail the accounting-by-product clause, it fails on **identity** (G-28) and on the Cycle-52 third test, because the aggregate is a public real-time by-product of the flow it would condition. **A state kind that is neither obligor-side nor aggregated-obligor side, and is published by a disinterested party, is now the register's precise standing condition for admissibility.**
- **The count of kills, stated plainly.** **Twelve named constructions across five entries** — R-063 (1), R-064 (3), R-065 (1), R-066 (3), R-067 (4) — plus the Cycles 115–121 state-kind screens, and **not one has produced a candidate**. Every kill names a clause or a template, and every kill is structural, and the cost of all twelve has been **zero sources** for the design-stage screens. That is a real efficiency and a real problem: the register has now demonstrated, five times over, that it can kill a construction it drew from its own memory, and has demonstrated nothing about whether it can find one.
- **What the route's unit of work must now change to.** The register-memory check is a **lower bound** on the space of published state kinds, and R-063's Route-1 finding (a) already established that **the register's own memory contains no institutional facts** — it contains mechanisms, templates and prior screens. A construction drawn from that memory is therefore *guaranteed* to be screened against a map built from the same memory, and the map has just been extended to twenty-eight rows. **The only instrument that can find a state kind outside the map is the literature — and the narrowed rule forbids consulting the literature before the construction passes the design-time screens.** That is a genuine methodological tension, not a rhetorical one, and it is the register's own answer to its standing open question about the *selection procedure*.
- **What would change the answer.** A construction becomes admissible again only when its state kind is **outside rows 1–28**, is published by a party **disinterested** in the flow **and** is not computed from the obligors' own records, is **sign-determining**, survives the accounting-by-product, anticipation and template screens, and has an unclaimed sign and a feasible clean test.
- **The honest limit of the map, unchanged.** The map remains a **lower bound**, not a proof of exhaustiveness; and a kind outside the register's prior art is invisible to a register-memory check. Cycle 126 adds the sharper form of that limit: **the register can no longer reach a new kind by working from its own memory**, because that is what the map is made of.

**Decision:** **bounded negative (R-067).** The four pre-registered measurement sets are killed at the design stage, with the killing clause or template named in each case and the obligor-side / disinterested-publisher-side / **aggregated-obligor-side** verdict stated in each case; **no dossier was created**; **no `IDEA-` identifier was consumed**; **no `S-` identifier was consumed by this screen (zero fetches)**; no score changed; no validation specification was added, amended or withdrawn; and **no new gap is opened**, because each kill is structural and independent of data availability. The screen's product is the **coverage map extended to twenty-eight rows** with **four new baseline rows (103–106)** as rediscovery guards, a **third structural category** named, and **one residual carried forward** (set (3)'s unnameable rescuing sub-variant) rather than closed.

---

## R-068 — The inverted route: the backward extraction of conditioning states from the register's own source ledger, and the closure of the adoption-count set (Cycle 128 — forty-four sources read, forty-four sources classified, **zero candidates**, three new state kinds closed, the **count-dilemma** named)

**What this entry is, and why the unit of work inverted.** Every screen in this register has worked **forwards**: draw a state kind from the register's memory, screen it, kill it. Cycle 126's closing section named the reason that route cannot reach a new kind — *the map is made of the same memory the constructions are drawn from*, so a construction is guaranteed to be screened against a map built from itself. Cycle 128 therefore inverted the unit of work: instead of asking *what state kind could I construct*, it asks **what state does the register's own corpus already condition on**. The instrument is the **source ledger itself** — 437 already-registered, already-verified sources — read **backwards**, one entry at a time, to recover the single conditioning state of the price effect each source claims. Nothing was fetched: this cycle consumed **no `S- identifier** and read **no new document**; the entire cost was reading what the register already holds.

**The pre-registration, restated.** The sample rule, the extraction rule and the four outcome categories were fixed in **Cycle 127** and are binding and unchanged; the Cycle-128 pre-registration in `cycle_notes.md` restates them and adds one sub-distinction inside outcome (iv). The sample is **every tenth source in ledger order** — a rule fixed *before* reading, so it cannot be cherry-picked afterwards — and the forty-four sampled entries are S-001 … S-201, S-221 … S-271 and S-291 … S-451. (The identifier arithmetic and the position arithmetic **coincide** here: the reserved-and-unused blocks S-210 … S-219 and S-281 … S-290 make the identifier slots S-211 and S-281 empty, so both readings of the sample rule select the same forty-four *existing* entries. This is recorded because the coincidence is a fact about the numbering, not an assumption.)

**The honesty qualification on "blind".** The Cycle-127 pre-registration requires the state to be recorded "as the source states it, **blind to the coverage map (rows 1–28)**". The twenty-eight-row map was already in this session's reading list, because the handoff instructs every agent to read R-067 and its map before acting. What the pre-registration protects is the *choice* of state — **one state per source, the state the effect is conditioned on, not every covariate mentioned** — and the *order* of operations, in which the in/out-of-map call is applied **after** the state is written down rather than chosen to fit a row. That is the procedure followed, and the qualification is stated here rather than asserted away. It is a real limit on the instrument and it is a limit on the *reading*, not on the *classification*: the classification is a lookup against a map fixed before the cycle.

### The extraction table — forty-four sources, one conditioning state each

The state is recorded **as the source states it**. "Already claimed?" asks whether the **price consequence** is already in the register's known-baseline table (narrowed rule (ii)). "In rows 1–28?" asks whether the **state kind** is inside the coverage map, with the Cycle-127 guard-rail applied: a state whose *family* is a known-baseline row is an **in-map null**, not a candidate.

### The distribution, stated plainly

**23 in-map nulls · 4 out-of-map and killed · 0 out-of-map survivors · 17 not-a-state** (of which **16 are (iv-a)** — sources that claim no price effect at all: rules, records, filings, data-access documents and programme pages — and **1 is (iv-b)**, S-021, whose price effect is conditioned on something that is **not a state at all**: managers' reputation concerns, which the source itself describes as a model outcome rather than a measured series). **Zero candidates. Zero `IDEA-` identifiers consumed. Zero `S-` identifiers consumed. Zero fetches.**

**The decision order that made the four categories exclusive — recorded, because the Cycle-127 wording of (iv-b) overlapped with (ii).** The pre-registration's (iv-b) clause read "a source whose price effect is conditioned on a state that is **not published**", which literally also covers a *measurable* state that is simply not published — an order-flow imbalance, an order-flow series — and those two sources are the most consequential rows of the extraction, because they are out-of-map *state kinds* that had to be screened and that extend the map. The order actually applied, and the reason for it, is: **(i)** if the state kind is inside rows 1–28, or its family is a known-baseline row → an **in-map null**; **(ii)** else if the source conditions a price effect on a **measurable** state kind outside rows 1–28 → out-of-map, and screened (a survivor would be (iii)); **(iv)** else → not-a-state, split into **(iv-a)** the source claims no price effect at all, and **(iv-b)** the effect is conditioned on something that is **not a state at all** (a private agent attribute, a model outcome). The tie-break is that a *measurable* state kind outside the map is a state, and must be screened and may extend the map, whereas a model outcome or a private attribute is not a state the register could ever screen. The pre-registration's wording is left as it was written; this is the record of how the overlap was resolved at the point of classification.

**What the distribution says, and it is the cycle's substantive finding.** Reading the register's own corpus backwards produces a **different population of states** from the one the forward screens have been hunting. The forward route asked for *published institutional records* and the map's twenty-eight rows are records. The backward route returns mostly states that are **not records at all**: microstructure quantities (order-flow imbalance, realised volatility), private routing attributes (the execution channel), country-level institutional character (the enforcement regime), model-internal states (reputation, strategic positioning). The corollary is the register's own methodological tension, now measured rather than argued: **the states the published literature actually conditions on are overwhelmingly not the states the register's constructions need.** Where a source's state *is* a published institutional record, it is (in 23 of 23 such cases) a state the map already holds and whose price consequence is already claimed — which is the register's standing rediscovery guard doing its work one source at a time.

### D1 — a real-time order-flow or order-book-imbalance state (S-011, S-051)

- **The construction, as extracted.** *State:* the order-flow imbalance in the limit order book (S-011), or the order flow itself (S-051) — both **computed from the tape**, not published as a record. *Flow:* the flow the imbalance measures. *Sign claim:* the sign of the price impact is the sign of the flow. *Instance candidates:* already registered and verified (S-011, S-051).
- **Register-memory check (before any literature).** The kind is **new to coverage rows 1–28** — it is not a price (row A), a fee, a count, a reserve, a breach, a waiver, a change of law or a clamp band, and it is not a published institutional record at all. The adjacent families are closed: **row 10** (commodity index roll: order-flow impact — the order-flow price effect in its best-documented institutional setting), **row 11** (liquidity provision around predictable large trades), **row 82** (flow price impact depends on the execution channel, not on the dollar) and **row 5** (short-term reversal and liquidity provision). Coverage row 19 (venue and regulatory trading constraints) does not reach a book-derived quantity, and template D (a published count or queue) does not reach it either, because nothing is *published* — it is **computed**.
- **Kill 1 — R-020's recorded principle, and it is the load-bearing one.** A state which would have to be **constructed** is not a published state. Order-flow imbalance is constructed from the limit order book at decision time; it is not a record any issuer, venue or regulator publishes. The register's own use of the word "published" is a hard requirement (G-13's sibling), and this fails it at the threshold.
- **Kill 2 — narrowed rule (i) and the Cycle-52 third test, together.** The imbalance **is** the flow: the state's numerator is the flow the candidate would trade, signed. It is therefore a public, real-time accounting by-product of the flow itself, and the Cycle-52 third test fails with the clause.
- **Kill 3 — G-12.** The sign of the impact is the sign of the flow, known a priori from the source's own statement. What the state adds is the **magnitude** — the impact coefficient falls with depth (S-011's own second conditioner) — and a magnitude is not a sign-determiner. R-041's reason 1 applies in its general form: a level sizes an effect rather than flipping its sign or its existence.
- **Verdict: bounded negative at the design stage; zero fetches.** This kind is **not a published institutional state**, and the register's forward map does not need a row for it — but it is recorded as a closed kind so that a later session does not re-derive it from the same two papers.

### D2 — a published settlement-based market-liquidity measurement (S-071)

- **The construction, as extracted.** *State:* a market-wide liquidity measure for the global currency market, computed from the settlement utility's own settlement records (S-071). *Flow:* the settlement flow the measure describes. *Sign claim:* the state is linked to currency risk premia and to volatility. *Instance candidate:* already registered and verified (S-071).
- **Register-memory check (before any literature).** The kind is **new to rows 1–28**. The adjacent families are closed: **row 28** (payment-system / CLS settlement deadlines as a price determinant), **row 29** (securities settlement cycle length as a price-impact and market-quality determinant), **row 30** (the post-US-close FX window as a market-quality object), **row 15** (the funding-currency / global-liquidity channel and FX carry crashes) and coverage row 20.
- **Kill 1 — the aggregated-obligor side (R-067's third structural category) and the Cycle-52 third test.** The measure is computed from **the participants' own settlement records** and is published by a disinterested utility; it is a public, real-time by-product of the very settlement flows it would condition on.
- **Kill 2 — the identity limb (G-28).** A market-wide liquidity measure is **not invertible** to the cross-sectional composition on which a price effect depends. The register's standing structural null applies: the quantity is published, the identity is not.
- **Kill 3 — narrowed rule (ii).** The price consequence — currency risk premia conditioned by a liquidity state — is a **published baseline** (row 15's family), so the constructed wedge is a published baseline.
- **Kill 4 — G-12.** A liquidity **level** sizes the effect; it does not flip its sign.
- **Verdict: bounded negative at the design stage; zero fetches.** **Aggregated-obligor side.**

### D3 — the execution channel through which an institutional flow is executed (S-331)

- **The construction, as extracted.** *State:* the vehicle or intermediary through which a given institutional flow is executed (mutual fund, separate account, transition manager). *Flow:* the institutional flow. *Sign claim:* the same dollar moves prices very differently through different vehicles, and a specialised intermediary cuts trading costs by 80–90 % with negligible price impact (S-331). *Instance candidate:* already registered and verified (S-331).
- **Register-memory check (before any literature).** The kind is **new to rows 1–28**: coverage row 19 (venue and regulatory trading constraints) does not reach a per-flow routing attribute, and template C does not reach it. The adjacent families are closed: **row 82** — which is the published price consequence of the channel, line-verified from this very source — and **row 46** (the closing-auction passive execution convention), and **row 63** (closing-price formation).
- **Kill 1 — R-020's recorded principle.** The channel is a **private routing decision**. It would have to be inferred from the flow's investor type, and inference is construction.
- **Kill 2 — narrowed rule (ii), and it is decisive.** Row 82 *is* this source's published price consequence, and it is already in the register's known-baseline table. The constructed wedge is a published baseline, so the construction cannot claim novelty on the price side either.
- **Kill 3 — G-12.** The *sign* of a channel's effect on a given flow depends on that flow's information content, urgency and the manager's discretion — all private, and none of them in the state.
- **Kill 4 — the wedge *is* the cost (R-066's D-3 kill 2 in the same shape).** Where the vehicles are substitutes the impact difference arbitrages away; where they are not, the sign depends on unpublished elasticities and depth.
- **Verdict: bounded negative at the design stage; zero fetches.** The kill is the same on the alternative reading (that the channel is a published structural fact, as in row 46): the price consequence is published either way, and the per-flow attribute is private either way.

### D4 — the adoption-count set (3)'s rescuing sub-variant: the named construction, the screen, and the **count-dilemma**

R-067's set (3) — *a published adoption / diffusion count* — was killed on every named branch, with **one residual carried forward**: the rescuing sub-variant could not be named, and the register's own rule is that a kill which cannot name a clause is not a kill. Cycle 128's pre-registration fixed the naming rule and the decision rule in advance, **before any literature was read**. This entry records the sub-variant as named, the screen, and the closure.

**The rescuing sub-variant, as named and pre-registered (blind to the literature).** *State:* a **published count of the firms that have adopted a decision** that is (a) not a fee, (b) not an execution convention, (c) not a tax-driven practice, (d) not part of IDEA-011's accounting-clock family, and (e) **whose adoption's price effect is not known a priori in sign** — a *contested-sign* decision. *Flow:* none mandated; a voluntary restructuring decision, whose timing is the issuer's own. *Wedge claimed:* the count conditions the *sign* of the market-level price reaction to that decision-type becoming widespread. *Instance candidate (named in advance, to be verified only if the construction survived the screen):* a standard-setter, regulator or index provider publishing a running count of adopters of a contested-sign corporate-structure decision (a dual-class share structure is the archetype: the price effect is contested in the literature — a discount in some studies, a premium in others).

**Register-memory check (before any literature).** The *kind* is a member of coverage **row 27** (a published adoption or diffusion count) — this is not a new state kind but a new member of an existing one. The adjacent families are closed: **row 20** (the fast-payment-rails family, whose recorded reason is exactly this one — "the family's decision-time object is a rail's *adoption curve* … the effect is a level shift in reserve demand that is repriced as it is learned"), **row 49** (the 10b5-1 adoption event's price effect is published), coverage row 10, and **IDEA-011's own family** (a diffusion count is the same mechanism as the accounting-clock candidate and is therefore a variant unless the counted object is a *different kind of decision* — which is exactly what condition (d) requires, and which the archetype does satisfy).

**Template screen.** **D matches directly** (R-041, baseline row 70: a published count or queue at decision date), and it matches on the *object* — a count — irrespective of what is counted. A count of adopters of a **mandated** practice is **template H** (R-045, row 74; a change of law published in advance, so anticipated). A count that crosses a published level to flip the effect's existence is **template I** (R-046, row 75).

**The screen under the narrowed rule, and the result.**

- **Kill 1 — the count-dilemma, case (a): the adoption's price effect is known a priori.** This is the ordinary case, and it is G-12 in its register-specific form. If the direction of the counted decision's price effect is known in advance (a technology's adoption raises demand for whatever the technology demands; a fee's adoption raises costs; a mandate's adoption creates a dated flow), then the count is a **monotone level**: it *sizes* the effect and cannot flip its sign or its existence. R-041's reason 1 applies word for word — a published count is a baseline level already embedded in published baselines — and so does R-064's D-2 reasoning, one level up. **A count cannot be a sign-determiner when the sign is already known.**
- **Kill 2 — the count-dilemma, case (b): the adoption's price effect is *not* known a priori.** This is the only branch that could rescue the set, and it fails the other way. If the sign is not known a priori, then the count does not contain the sign: the sign depends on the **balance of offsetting effects**, or on **which** adopters, and the count contains neither. A state that does not determine the sign is not a sign-determiner, so the strict bar fails; and recovering the sign would require **constructing** the balance, which is R-020's recorded principle again — a state that would have to be constructed is not a published state. **The two cases are exhaustive, and they kill in opposite directions: in case (a) the count cannot flip the sign, in case (b) the count cannot contain the sign.**
- **Kill 3 — template D, on the object.** Independently of the dilemma, a published count is a published count (R-041), and the register's template programme exists precisely to stop a construction whose state is one of the nine template kinds **at zero cost**.
- **Kill 4 — the threshold variant is template I** (R-046's reason 1): a count that flips the effect's existence at a level is a deterministic function of an already-published number.
- **Kill 5 — the mandated variant is template H** (R-045's reason 1): a count of adopters of a *mandated* practice is published in advance, so it is anticipated, and the flow is the obligor's own.
- **Kill 6 — narrowed rule (i) and the Cycle-52 third test.** Whichever publisher is imagined — a standard-setter, a regulator, an index provider, an industry body — the count is **computed from the adopters' own filings and notices**. It is the aggregated-obligor side (R-067's third structural category) in its purest form: a published, lagged by-product of the very decisions it would condition on.
- **Kill 7 — narrowed rule (ii).** The price consequence of a decision-type's diffusion — whether the effect of a corporate-structure decision, a disclosure practice, a technology adoption or a mandate adoption on prices is a *cross-sectional* or a *market-level* relation — is published (rows 20, 49 and the IDEA-011 family's prior art), and in the contested-sign case the cross-sectional price relation is published too, in both directions. The constructed wedge is a published baseline in every branch.
- **The self-consistency check against the register's own prior record, run in this cycle.** The count-dilemma is a *generalisation* of what the register already held, and the two must not contradict each other. **Baseline row 70** (the template-D screen, R-041) records, in its own words, that "**the count alone does not constitute an unclaimed sign-determiner (fails G-12)**" and that "any claimed threshold is anticipated or already treated as a friction/baseline" — which is **case (a)** of the dilemma, stated for one screen; and **R-041's reason 1** states that a published count is a baseline level already embedded in published baselines, so it sizes an effect rather than flipping its sign or its existence. The dilemma therefore **extends** the register's recorded position from one screen to the whole class, and **contradicts nothing**: the only case the dilemma adds is **case (b)** — a count whose *sign* is not known a priori — and the register had no recorded reason against that case, because no screen had posed it. The count-dilemma's standing against the register's own record is therefore: **consistent, and narrower in its claim than the register's screens, which were per-kind.**
- **Verdict: bounded negative; the residual is closed, not carried.** The sub-variant **is** nameable, and it is killed by **seven named clauses and templates**. R-067's set (3) is therefore a **complete kill**: there is no live construction left in it. The handoff's expectation that a killed sub-variant would add a **twenty-ninth row** to the coverage map is recorded here as **not what happened, and why**: the rescuing sub-variant is a **member of the existing kind at row 27**, not a new kind, so the correct action is to **close row 27 in full**, with the count-dilemma as its closing reason. A new row would have been a duplicate of a kind the map already holds, which is the register's own standing defect to avoid.
- **What the count-dilemma is, precisely, and what it is not.** It is a **logical result about a class of states**, not a fourth provenance category: the obligor-side / disinterested-publisher-side / aggregated-obligor-side taxonomy classifies *where a published state comes from*, whereas the count-dilemma says that a class of states — **counts** — is self-killing, because a count is a level whose content cannot be a sign-determiner. It is stated in the register's own vocabulary (G-12's sign-versus-magnitude ordering, R-041's reason 1, R-046's reason 1, R-045's reason 1, R-020's recorded principle, the Cycle-52 third test) and it depends on **no fact outside the register**, which is what the Cycle-128 pre-registration required of any such argument. **It is not** a claim that adoption counts are useless as *descriptive* measures, and it is not a claim that no published count can ever be a state — a count that a rule publishes as a *threshold* (template I) or a count that a regulator publishes as an *aggregate of a private quantity* (row 104) are different objects, and both are already closed by their own entries.

### The residual, stated honestly — what the inverted route produced, and what it did not

- **The count of kills.** **Fifteen named constructions across six entries** — R-063 (1), R-064 (3), R-065 (1), R-066 (3), R-067 (4), R-068 (3 out-of-map kinds plus the closure of set (3)) — plus the Cycles 115–121 state-kind screens, and **not one has produced a candidate**. The cost of all fifteen remains **zero sources**.
- **The route's product is different in kind from the forward route's.** The forward route produced a *map of closed kinds*. The backward route produced a **measurement of the register's own corpus**: twenty-three of the forty-four sampled sources condition their price effect on a state the map already holds, and in **every one of those twenty-three** the price consequence is already claimed; the remaining twenty-one split into seventeen sources that claim no price effect at all **(sixteen)** or condition on an unpublished state **(one)**, and four whose state kind is outside the map — and **all four of those were killed by named clauses**. **Cycle-135 correction to this sentence (before → after: "(fifteen) … (two)" → "(sixteen) … (one)"):** this sentence's parenthetical split contradicted **this entry's own** distribution line above — "**16 are (iv-a)** … and **1 is (iv-b)**, S-021" — and **this entry's own** forty-four-row extraction table, which carries **sixteen (iv-a)** cells and **one (iv-b)** cell (S-021). The two totals (seventeen not-a-state; twenty-one remainder) were correct; only the split between the two sub-categories was mis-stated, by one in each direction. The finding is unchanged: no candidate, no identifier consumed, no fetch. **No score, rank, flag, gap or specification changed.** The register's corpus is, in this sample, **internally consistent with its own rediscovery guard**.
- **The one substantive new fact about the register's method.** The backward route does not find new *kinds*; it finds a **different population of states** — computed, private and model-internal ones. That is a quantitative statement of the tension R-067 recorded qualitatively: the states the published literature conditions on are mostly not the states the register's constructions need, and the map's rows are all *records* because the forward route only ever looked for records. **The register's own literature contains fourteen of its own rejected families' states** and uses them as controls, not as candidates.
- **The honest limit, sharpened.** The sample is **forty-four of 437 sources (10.1 %)**, fixed by position, and the corpus is a corpus of *literature the register chose to read* — it is not a sample of the space of published institutional records, and the register has no instrument that makes it one. The map therefore remains a **lower bound**, and the backward route does not change that; what it changes is that the *lower bound* has now been probed from the other direction, at a cost of zero fetches, and has not been raised.
- **What would change the answer.** A construction becomes admissible again only when its state kind is **outside rows 1–31**, is published by a party **disinterested** in the flow **and** is not computed from the obligors' own records, is **sign-determining**, survives the accounting-by-product, anticipation and template screens, and has an unclaimed sign and a feasible clean test. The register's standing difficulty is unchanged and is now stated with a number attached: the *only* instrument that can reach a state kind outside the map is the literature, and the narrowed rule forbids the literature until the construction passes screens that are themselves built from the map.

**Decision:** **bounded negative (R-068).** The backward extraction of forty-four sources produced **no candidate**; **three out-of-map state kinds** were surfaced and each was killed at the design stage with a **named clause**, and is recorded as a closed kind (rows 29–31 of the coverage map; baseline rows 107–109); the **adoption-count set (3)'s carried-forward residual is closed** by D4's named sub-variant and the **count-dilemma**, so **R-067's set (3) is a complete kill and row 27 of the map is closed in full**. **No dossier was created**; **no `IDEA-` identifier was consumed**; **no `S-` identifier was consumed and no fetch was made**; no score changed; no validation specification was created, amended or withdrawn; **no new gap is opened**, because each kill is structural and independent of data availability. The screen's product is the **coverage map extended to thirty-one rows**, **three new baseline rows (107–109)**, **one baseline row amended in place (105)** and the register's first **measurement of its own corpus against its own rediscovery guard**.
