## Cycle 130 batch, re-derived per source in Cycle 131 (S-051 … S-075)

**Why this table exists.** Cycle 130 recorded this batch **only as a one-line summary** — "25 in-map nulls (funding-liquidity / FX-fixing / settlement state family, all already in coverage map rows); 0 out-of-map; 0 not-a-state (every entry in this slice claims a price effect or a documented mechanism)". The entries were **re-read from the ledger in Cycle 131** and are written out per source, so that the batch is a record rather than a claim. **Zero fetches; the instrument is the register's own ledger.**

**The convention this re-derivation applies, stated so that it can be checked.** Category (iv-a) is applied when the entry claims **no consequence of its own** — no price, rate, return or valuation consequence — which is R-068's test and which explicitly covers **data-access documents, records, filings, rules and programme pages**. Where an entry describes a *mechanism* or an *amplification* of a market condition, that is itself a claimed consequence, and the entry is classified in-map against the state the mechanism names. The Cycle-130 summary applied a **looser** test ("a price effect **or a documented mechanism**"), which is why the two records differ on four entries; the difference is recorded here, and the register adopts the stricter reading.

The two entries that Cycle 128 had already read (S-051, S-071) are re-read here and agree with R-068's own rows in substance: both were killed as out-of-map kinds in Cycle 128 and both kinds now stand as **closed rows 29 and 30** of the map, so a re-reading of the same sources finds them in-map.

## Cycle 131 partial state inventory — batch 1 (S-076 … S-125)

Pre-registered batch boundary: 50 consecutive ledger entries, S-076 … S-125 (the Cycle-128 sampled identifiers S-081, S-091, S-101, S-111 and S-121 fall inside the range and were **re-derived and compared** with R-068's own rows). Extraction rule unchanged from R-068: one conditioning state per source, as the source states it; plus sign claim, already-claimed status, in/out-of-map status. **Zero fetches; the instrument is the register's own ledger.**

**The convention this table uses, stated so that the columns can be checked.** In the "in/out-of-map" column, **map row** means a row of the **published-state-kind coverage map** (A–I and 10–33), and **template** means one of the register's nine named state templates; in the "already claimed?" column, **baseline row** means a row of the **known-baseline table** (1–111). The two numberings are different tables and are never mixed. The **stricter (iv-a) test** adopted in Cycle 131 applies: an entry that documents a state but claims **no price, rate, return or valuation consequence of its own** is (iv-a) not-a-state, even where it documents a state that *would* condition a price effect.

**Outcome of batch 1: 33 in-map, 0 out-of-map, 0 out-of-map survivors, 16 not-a-state (iv-a) and 1 not-a-state (iv-b) — S-079.** **No out-of-map state kind, no candidate, no `IDEA-` identifier, no `S-` identifier, no fetch, no score change, no gap opened or closed, no specification created or amended.** The **five** sampled identifiers in the range were re-derived and **all five agree with R-068's own rows** (S-081, S-091, S-101, S-111, S-121); the only refinement is that S-081 is mapped to map rows 10 **and** 12, where R-068 cited row 10 alone.

**Three classifications in this batch are borderline, and the register states its reading rather than hiding it.** (i) **S-079** is (iv-b) not-a-state rather than (ii) out-of-map: the entry's conditioning object is a *model-internal assumption about order clustering*, which is the pre-registered (iv-b) test — "a private agent attribute" — and not a published state of the world; if a later cycle reads the clustering as a measurable order-book state, it must re-read this entry against map row 29 (the closed kind D1) and record the change. (ii) **S-087** is (iv-a): its "arbitrage pricing … the futures price coincides with spot" is a **pricing identity** of a published rule, not a claimed price *effect* conditioned on a published state, and the entry's own use of it in the register is institutional context. (iii) **S-102** is (iv-a) on the ground that the statutory text claims no price, rate, return or valuation consequence of its own; the price-inelasticity statement in the entry is the register's interpretation of the penalty's size, and the register records it as such rather than as the source's claim.

**The mechanical count, recorded so that the "already claimed?" column can be checked.** The column asks whether the **price consequence** is already claimed in the register, and the claim is often made in a **dossier or a shortlist hard-stop note** rather than in the known-baseline table. The narrower, mechanical test — *does the source's own identifier appear in the known-baseline table?* — gives: **absent** for S-078, S-079, S-083, S-084, S-085, S-088, S-089, S-090, S-091, S-092, S-100, S-102, S-103, S-104, S-105, S-106, S-107, S-108, S-109, S-110, S-111 and S-116 (twenty-two entries, of which twelve are (iv-a) entries and one is the (iv-b) entry, whose *state* is documented in a baseline row without the source being cited, and nine are in-map entries whose price consequence is claimed in a dossier or a hard-stop note — the short-sale-constraint and lending-market family in IDEA-008's dossier, the funding family at baseline rows 76 and 93, the funding-currency channel at baseline row 15, the 0DTE-share family at baseline rows 13 and 111, the EU ETS family at baseline rows 22 and 31 through S-101, and the order-flow price-impact law embedded in baseline rows 5, 10 and 82); **present** for the remaining twenty-eight. **The same conclusion holds as in batches 2 and 3**: the corpus and the rediscovery guard are consistent at the level of the **price consequence**, and the baseline table is not the only place a consequence is claimed.

**A mechanical count, recorded so that the "already claimed?" column can be checked.** The column asks whether the **price consequence** is already claimed in the register, and the claim is often made in a **dossier or a shortlist hard-stop note** rather than in the known-baseline table. The narrower, mechanical test — *does the source's own identifier appear in the known-baseline table?* — gives: **absent** for S-126, S-127, S-128, S-129, S-130, S-131, S-132, S-136, S-152, S-169 and S-175 (eleven entries, of which eight are (iv-a) entries whose *state* is documented in a baseline row without the source being cited, and three are in-map entries — S-131, S-152 and S-175 — whose price consequence is claimed in IDEA-011's dossier and in the index-methodology family rather than in the baseline table); **present** for the remaining thirty-nine. The two counts are consistent, and the difference is the point: **the register's corpus and its rediscovery guard are consistent at the level of the price consequence, even where the baseline table does not name the source.**

## The state inventory as a whole — and where the map-vs-corpus audit is recorded

**The instrument.** Every table above reads the register's **own source ledger** backwards, one entry at a time, for the single conditioning state of the published price effect the entry claims. **Zero fetches, zero new sources, zero new citations** — the whole cost of the inventory so far is reading what the register already holds.

**The arithmetic of the inventory, as of the end of Cycle 131.** Raw readings **257**; **20** double-reads (the sampled identifiers re-derived inside a batch and compared with R-068's own rows — every one of them agrees in substance); **237 distinct sources inventoried out of the ledger's 437**, i.e. **54.2 %** of the corpus, with **200 entries (45.8 %) not yet inventoried**. The distribution over the 237 distinct sources: **170 in-map nulls · 0 out-of-map survivors · 0 out-of-map-and-killed-not-yet-mapped (all six killed kinds now stand as closed map rows 29–33) · 67 not-a-state (65 (iv-a), 2 (iv-b))**. At fifty entries per batch, **four further batches** complete the inventory.

**Where the audit itself is recorded.** The pre-registered **map-vs-corpus audit with numbers** — the corpus count, the distinct-source count, the un-inventoried ranges, the distribution and the fraction of the corpus the map has been probed against — is written out in **`cycle_notes.md`, Cycle 131, Priority 3(c)**, together with the standing limit: the corpus is a corpus of literature the register chose to read, so **the map remains a lower bound**.

## Cycle 134 partial state inventory — final batch (S-439 … S-457)

Pre-registered batch boundary: 19 identifier slots, S-439 … S-457, of which **17 are new reads** (S-439, S-440, S-442 … S-457) and **two are double-reads** (S-441 and S-451, both of which the Cycle-128 sample had already read). Extraction rule unchanged from R-068: one conditioning state per source, as the source states it; plus sign claim, already-claimed status, in/out-of-map status. **Zero fetches; the instrument is the register's own ledger.** The convention: map row means published-state-kind coverage map (A–I, 10–33); baseline row means known-baseline table (1–111). Stricter (iv-a) test applies (documentation with no price consequence claimed is (iv-a), even if it documents a state that would condition a price effect).

**Why this batch exists — the pre-registered verification, and its result.** The Cycle-133 handoff asserted that "all 437 sources have been inventoried" and that "no further backward extraction" was needed. The Cycle-134 verification was run **mechanically** — the ledger's entry blocks were enumerated, the identifiers appearing in the inventory tables were enumerated, and the two were differenced — and the assertion is **false**. The difference is this batch: **seventeen ledger entries had never been read** for their conditioning state, because the ledger grew by nineteen entries after the batches were pre-registered (S-439 … S-447 at Cycle 114, S-448 … S-453 at Cycle 123, S-454 and S-455 at Cycle 125, S-456 and S-457 at Cycle 126) while the extraction stopped at S-438. This batch therefore **finishes the inventory**; it does not extend the route.

## The state inventory as a whole — final state (end of Cycle 134)

**The instrument, unchanged.** Every table above reads the register's **own source ledger** backwards, one entry at a time, for the single conditioning state of the published price effect the entry claims. **Zero fetches, zero new sources, zero new citations** across the whole route (Cycles 128–134).

**The arithmetic, re-derived from the files in Cycle 134 (not carried forward).**

- **The corpus.** `source_ledger.md` holds **447 entry blocks**: **437** `### S-NNN` headings **plus the ten Cycle-58 document-record entries S-281 … S-290**, which carry no `###` heading of their own. **S-290 is unverifiable and struck** (the Cycle-58 state-integrity repair), so the corpus is **447 entries, of which 446 are verifiable**.
- **The inventory.** **447 of 447 distinct entries have now been read and classified** — **100 % of the corpus**. The register's earlier figure of "437 of 437 (100 %)" was false in both the numerator and the denominator: the denominator was ten short (the ten Cycle-58 entries had been mis-declared as reserved and unused since Cycle 114) and the numerator was seventeen short (the nineteen entries added after Cycle 133's batches were pre-registered had never been read).
- **The distribution over the 447 distinct entries:** **272 in-map · 0 out-of-map survivors · 0 out-of-map-and-killed-not-yet-mapped · 172 not-a-state (168 (iv-a), 4 (iv-b))**. The three entries whose cell reads "out-of-map" — **S-011** (order-flow imbalance), **S-027** (investor attention) and **S-043** (0DTE volume share) — were **all killed**, and they stand as closed map rows **29, 32 and 33** respectively; the three out-of-map kinds found by the Cycle-128 sample (a real-time order-flow or order-book-imbalance state; a published settlement-based market-liquidity measurement; the execution channel through which an institutional flow is executed) were each killed by a named clause and are closed map rows **29–31**, with S-051, S-071 and S-331 consequently re-read as in-map. **There is no out-of-map survivor anywhere in the corpus.** The four (iv-b) entries — **S-021, S-028, S-079, S-167** — are the sources that condition a price effect on something that is not a published state (a model-internal reputation state; a return decomposition; a private order-placement attribute; private contract terms).
- **The double-reads.** Every sampled identifier re-read inside a batch has been compared with R-068's own row; **all agree in substance**, and the three disagreements found are recorded above with their corrections: the ten Cycle-58 entries (**S-281 … S-290**, which R-068's sample never touched and which Cycle 133 described as reserved and empty — corrected in place); **S-028** (labelled "out-of-map" by Cycle 129 with no kill and no map row behind it — re-classified (iv-b)); and **S-021** (R-068 (iv-b) against the batch's in-map — the stricter reading adopted, per the Cycle-131 convention, and the disagreement recorded rather than adopted silently).
- **The honest limit, unchanged.** The corpus is a corpus of literature the register chose to read, so the map remains a **lower bound**, not a proof of exhaustiveness; and a published state kind outside the register's prior art remains invisible to a register-memory check.

**What the completed inventory does and does not establish.** It establishes that **the register's own corpus contains no published state kind that the register has not already killed, mapped or recorded as a baseline** — the rediscovery guard holds at the level of the corpus. It does **not** establish that the corpus is exhaustive of the literature; the map remains a lower bound, and the corpus's own selection is the limit.
